## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:35:45.294Z

Tracing the revision timeline: the goal was set at 21:25 and revision climbed through plan derivation and provider updates, but the retry at 11:30:29 already shows revision 8 in an error state, meaning that snapshot reflects a failure recorded after the fact — so the goal was likely already paused or errored before that retry occurred. I'm now trying to pin down the actual root cause of the failure.

The pattern is clear: the `/goal` slash-command fallback calls `askAgent`, which hard-fails with 409 when the worker is mid-turn, and that failure gets recorded as a transport failure that burns the goal to error — so retrying while the worker is still busy just repeats the same failure.

Now I want to design a fix that treats "agent busy" as a deferral rather than a transport failure. I'm thinking of adding a deferred result type distinguishing no active lease from worker busy, and wrapping the slash-command dispatch so that a busy-agent error returns this deferred state instead of failing outright. I also need to check whether the extension path through `invokeAgentAcpMethod` hits the same busy check, since the busy-detection logic currently seems scoped only to `askAgent` and I need to verify whether `invokeAcpMethod` shares that guard.

For retrying, I want a way to re-dispatch a deferred goal control once the worker goes idle, checking whether the goal's control method is still null and a lease exists, calling this at the point in sync.ts where a turn settles for direct runs. This condition — control_method being null — matches the same check used at startup recovery, so I can pull that logic into a shared helper. I need to be careful since bumping the plan revision changes the row, though the coordinator re-reads the latest goal state regardless. Finally, I need to think about how to unburn any goals already stuck in the error state and surface a clearer message to the frontend.

I should check GoalPlanManager, runtime-api goal call, and GoalPlanCard to see how deferred status is surfaced in the UI, and also look for an existing test suite covering goal-acp. Let me gather all of this in parallel.

interface GoalPlanCardProps {
  goal: GoalSnapshot | null;

  onOpenPlanArtifact?: (uri: string) => void;

function elapsedLabel(startedAt: string, endAt: string | null, nowMs: number) {
  const start = new Date(startedAt).getTime();
  const end = endAt ? new Date(endAt).getTime() : nowMs;
  const seconds = Math.max(0, Math.floor((end - start) / 1_000));
  if (seconds < 60) return t("goal.elapsed.seconds", { count: seconds });
  const minutes = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  if (minutes < 60) return t("goal.elapsed.minutes", { count: minutes });
  const hours = Math.floor(minutes / 60);
  if (hours < 24) return t("goal.elapsed.hours", { count: hours });
  return t("goal.elapsed.days", { count: Math.floor(hours / 24) });
}

/**
 * States a goal can sit in while nothing is driving it forward.
 *
 * A goal stalls in far more ways than `paused`: the agent can block it, park it
 * on the user, lose its goal capability, or fail outright, and it can sit in
 * `pending` because the agent never picked it up. The card previously offered a
 * resume control only for `paused`, and only when the agent advertised a resume
 * capability — which left every other stalled goal with no way back to
 * `pursuing` at all.
 */
const STALLED_GOAL_STATUSES = new Set<GoalStatus>([
  "pending",
  "paused",
  "waiting_user",
  "blocked",
  "limited",
  "error",
]);

/**
 * The single play/pause control in the card header.
 *
 * `retry` rather than `resume` is what a stalled goal without an advertised
 * resume capability sends: both land on `pursuing`, but only `retry` is exempt
 * from the capability check, so it still works for an agent that reports no
 * goal capabilities at all.
 */
export function resolveGoalRunControl(goal: GoalSnapshot): { action: GoalMutationAction; labelKey: string } | null {
  if (goal.status === "pursuing") {
    return goal.capabilities.pause ? { action: "pause", labelKey: "goal.action.pause" } : null;
  }
  if (!STALLED_GOAL_STATUSES.has(goal.status)) return null;
  return {
    action: goal.capabilities.resume ? "resume" : "retry",
    labelKey: "goal.action.resume",
  };
}

function GoalIconButton({
  label,
  children,
  className,
  ...props
}: React.ComponentProps<typeof Button> & { label: string }) {
  return (
    <Tooltip>
      <TooltipTrigger render={<Button type="button" size="icon-sm" variant="ghost" aria-label={label} title={label} className={className} {...props} />}>
        {children}
      </TooltipTrigger>
      <TooltipContent>{label}</TooltipContent>
    </Tooltip>
  );
}

  setActionError(actionError: GoalPlanActionError | null) {
    this.setKey("actionError", actionError);
  }

  setClearConfirmation(runId: string, open: boolean) {
    this.setKey("confirmingClearRunId", open ? runId : null);
  }

  beginOperation(args: Omit<GoalPlanPendingOperation, "operationId"> & { operationId?: string }) {
    if (this.getSnapshot().pending) return null;
    const pending = { ...args, operationId: args.operationId ?? createOperationId() };
    this.patch({ pending, failedOperation: null, actionError: null });
    return pending;
  }

  async executeOperation(args: {
    goalsApi: RuntimeAPIs["goals"];
    operation: GoalPlanPendingOperation;
    onSnapshot: (snapshot: GoalSnapshot, eventKey: string | null) => void;
  }): Promise<{ ok: true; snapshot: GoalSnapshot } | { ok: false }> {
    const { goalsApi, operation, onSnapshot } = args;
    try {
      const response = operation.action === "edit" || operation.action === "set"
        ? await goalsApi.put({
            runId: operation.runId,
            body: {
              goalId: operation.goalId,
              expectedRevision: operation.baseRevision,
              operationId: operation.operationId,
              objective: operation.objective ?? "",
            },
          })
        : await goalsApi.act({
            runId: operation.runId,
            body: {
              goalId: operation.goalId,
              expectedRevision: operation.baseRevision,
              operationId: operation.operationId,
              action: operation.action,
            },
          });
      const snapshot = parseGoalSnapshot((response as { goal?: unknown }).goal);
      onSnapshot(snapshot, null);
      this.completeOperation(operation.runId, operation.operationId, snapshot.revision);
      return { ok: true, snapshot };
    } catch (error) {
      const newest = operationErrorSnapshot(error);
      if (newest) onSnapshot(newest, null);
      this.failOperation(
        operation.runId,
        operation.operationId,
        operationErrorKind(error),
        newest ? { goalId: newest.goalId, revision: newest.revision } : undefined,
      );
      return { ok: false };
    }
  }

  completeOperation(runId: string, operationId: string, serverRevision: number) {
    const pending = this.getSnapshot().pending;
    if (!pending || pending.runId !== runId || pending.operationId !== operationId) return false;
    if (serverRevision < pending.baseRevision) return false;
    this.patch({
      pending: null,
      failedOperation: null,
      actionError: null,
      editingRunId: null,
      editDraft: "",
      focusRunId: runId,
      confirmingClearRunId: null,
    });
    return true;
  }

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:36:14.410Z

I need to check how the UI handles the deferred case and run status, since the worker is mid-turn here. The user's real intent is to resume the goal, and since the worker is busy, deferring the slash command until turn end is the right approach, consistent with the queued-message path. Now I'm sketching the changes needed in goal-acp.ts, starting with extending the dispatch result type.

For catching busy errors, I need an isAgentBusyError check, but the existing one in queued-messages.ts pulls in heavy db dependencies I'd rather avoid importing into goal-acp. I'm considering adding a lighter version to retry.ts alongside the existing isMissingAgentError check instead. For goal-control-dispatch.ts, the outcome type already unions in the new dispatch result, so the deferred reason should flow through naturally in the converge loop. And in the goals.ts route, dispatchAndPublish emits a reconciliation-refused event on deferred while still returning 200, so the goal won't get burned to an error state, which is the behavior I want.

I do want the goal to reflect that control is pending though — right now after a retry action the status would show "pursuing" even though the agent hasn't actually been notified, but this matches how no_active_lease already behaves and gets corrected during reconciliation, so it's acceptable. For retrying on turn settle, I'm planning to extract a per-run reconcilePendingGoalControl(runId) function out of the existing startup recovery logic, then call it from sync.ts right where refreshDerivedGoalPlan runs on turn_settled — though I need to be careful since sync.ts ticks over all runs, so I want to make sure this reconcile call doesn't get triggered redundantly across the loop.

I'm scoping the reconcile query the same way as the startup recovery query — filtering on control_method being null with a worker and session present — so it exits early when there's nothing pending. But I'm now second-guessing whether control_method actually gets reset to null when a goal revision bumps, since markControlApplied only sets it while it's still null, so if putGoal or actionGoal don't clear it on bump, this whole reconciliation path could be silently broken.

I want to grep for control_method in goal-control.ts to confirm. I'm also thinking about a related edge case: whether an error-state goal (from a busy failure) properly transitions back to pursuing on retry before redispatch, and whether the deferred response returning 200 without an error banner still needs some user-facing indication that a retry will happen.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:36:24.981Z

Good, so control_method is reset to NULL on putGoal, actionGoal, and applyProviderUpdate, and only set to a specific value when a failure is recorded — meaning NULL correctly signals a pending dispatch, and the busy-deferred case leaves it NULL too. Now I'm tracing the current bug scenario where control_method gets set to 'transport'.

With my fix, the busy path won't call recordControlFailure anymore, so control_method stays NULL and retries work correctly. Now I need to sort out where isAgentBusyError should live — it's currently duplicated across queued-messages.ts, refresh.ts, and bridge-client.ts, and the cleanest fix is consolidating it into the shared retry.ts module (alongside isMissingAgentError) and having queued-messages re-export it to avoid multiple definitions.

```text
function isNonRetryableBridgeFailureDetail(detail: string) {
  // `no_pending_*` means the runtime already resolved that request (the user
  // answered from another surface, or the turn was cancelled). Retrying can
  // only steal the *next* request's slot, and it delays the conflict the
  // caller needs to see.
  return /\bAgent session did not include a session id\b/i.test(detail)
    || /\bno_pending_(?:elicitations|permissions)\b/i.test(detail);
}

function isAgentBusyError(error: unknown) {
  return /\bagent is busy\b/i.test(describeError(error));
}

function workerTurnSupersededError(name: string) {
  return Object.assign(
    new Error(`Worker turn superseded by a newer worker turn: ${name}`),
    { code: "WORKER_TURN_SUPERSEDED", retryable: false },
  );
}

function asString(value: unknown, fallback = "") {
  return typeof value === "string" ? value : fallback;
}

function asNullableString(value: unknown) {
SEP
      }
      const res = await fetch(`${BRIDGE_URL}${path}`, {
        method: "POST",
        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
        body: JSON.stringify(body),
        ...(signal ? { signal } : {}),
      });
      if (!res.ok) {
        let detail = `${res.status} ${res.statusText}`;
        try {
          const payload = await res.json() as { error?: unknown };
          if (typeof payload.error === "string" && payload.error.trim()) {
            detail = payload.error.trim();
          }
        } catch {
          // ignore malformed/non-json bodies and fall back to status text
        }
        throw Object.assign(new Error(`Ask failed: ${detail}`), {
          status: res.status,
          retryable: isAgentBusyError(detail) ? false : undefined,
        });
      }
      await options.onAccepted?.();
      const result = await readAskStream(res, signal);
      // Some runtimes acknowledge cancellation with a normal terminal frame
      // instead of a connection error. Fence that late success as well.
      await assertTurnIsCurrent();
      return result;
    }, {
      maxDelayMs: BRIDGE_CONNECTION_RESET_MAX_BACKOFF_MS,
      operationLabel: `Ask /agents/${name}/ask`,
      // An aborted turn must die on the spot. Without this, an abort mid-stream
      // reads as a connection failure and the "retry indefinitely" path revives
      // the very turn the user just stopped.
      retryIndefinitelyWhen: (error) =>
        !isWorkerTurnAbortedError(error)
        && isRecoverableConnectionSupervisorError(error)
        && !isBridgeConnectionRefused(error),
    });
  } catch (error) {
    if (isWorkerTurnSupersededError(error) || isWorkerTurnAbortedError(error)) {
      throw error;
    }
    if (isBridgeConnectionRefused(error)) {
      throw new Error(
        `OmniHarness agent runtime is not running at ${BRIDGE_URL}. Start it with pnpm dev or ` +
        `pnpm exec tsx scripts/agent-runtime.ts. Original error: ${describeError(error)}`,
      );
    }

    const detail = describeError(error);
    const normalizedDetail = stripRepeatedActionPrefix(detail, "Ask");

    throw new Error(`Ask failed: ${normalizedDetail}`);
  }
}

export async function getAgent(name: string, options: { retryIndefinitely?: boolean } = {}) {
  const agent = await requestBridge<unknown>(`/agents/${name}`, {}, "Get agent", options);
  return normalizeAgentRecord(agent);
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:36:53.936Z

I'll add `isAgentBusyError` to retry.ts and have queued-messages.ts re-export it from there instead of defining it separately, since grep shows it's not used elsewhere including tests.

I should also wrap the extension invocation path with the same busy-check, since the underlying provider could still reject even though invokeAcpMethod doesn't check busy itself—wrapping both is cheap and consistent.

Now I'm writing a function that reconciles pending goal control by querying run_goals for a run with no control method set but with a worker and ACP session assigned...

Wait, I need to reconsider: if I call this reconciliation after every turn settle in sync.ts, it could misfire whenever control_method gets nulled for reasons other than a genuinely pending user request—like when applyProviderUpdate bumps revision and nulls control_method after a provider-side update. That would cause us to echo a `/goal` command back to the agent that it never actually needed, creating a feedback loop. The startup recovery function only runs once so it doesn't have this problem, but wiring this into the per-turn sync path needs a guard to distinguish "genuinely pending because nothing has dispatched it yet" from "just nulled by reconciliation" before I re-dispatch.

So instead of relying on the NULL control_method as a signal, I'm thinking of tracking deferrals explicitly in memory: the `GoalControlDispatchCoordinator` marks a runId as pending whenever a dispatch attempt is deferred specifically due to the worker being busy, and clears that mark once a later dispatch resolves one way or another. Then a `reconcileDeferredGoalControl` function checks this in-memory set at turn settle to decide whether to retry, which avoids the echo risk while still covering the transient busy case, with startup recovery handling anything lost across a restart.

To retry correctly I want to re-derive the right action from the goal's current status via `recoveryAction` rather than blindly replaying the original request -- so a goal now `pursuing` resolves to "set" and re-dispatches `/goal <objective>`, while `paused` resolves to "pause" and sends `/goal pause`. Since state might have shifted between the deferral and the retry, I'm leaning toward storing the snapshot and action together and letting `converge`'s existing fence comparison handle any staleness when the coordinator re-dispatches.

Now I need to decide where to wire this in. The natural spot is the `turn_settled` handler in sync.ts for direct runs, since goal cards and control only appear for direct/conversation sessions, not planning or implementation runs. I'll add the hook there, though I'm weighing whether it also needs to cover the persisted-direct-completion path.

I should also emit a named event for the deferral and retry, consistent with existing lifecycle observability -- `goal.reconciliation.refused` already covers the deferred case, so retries can flow through the existing completed/failed events, but I need to check the typed event-kind union to see if new kinds are needed.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:37:38.805Z

I'll add an `isAgentBusyError` export in retry.ts alongside the existing missing-agent check, then have queued-messages import and re-export it instead of keeping its own local definition, to avoid duplicating the logic.

I'm checking for circular import risk—retry.ts only pulls from provider-account-failures, so it's safe to import into queued-messages. Now I'm updating goal-acp.ts to define a dispatch result type covering dispatched, deferred, and unsupported outcomes, and wiring the dispatch logic to catch errors from invokeExtension and check them against isAgentBusyError.

I'm applying the same deferred-on-busy pattern to the slash command fallback path, then moving to goal-control-dispatch.ts to add a deferral map keyed by run for tracking snapshots and pending mutation actions, along with a coordinator that manages a sequential tail of convergence work per run.

Working through the convergence logic, I want it to record a deferral when dispatch comes back busy, clear it when the run succeeds or gets superseded, and I'm now considering restructuring so the clear happens at the top of convergence instead of scattered across each branch.

Adding retry helper methods...

Handling the branch paths for unsupported, deferred, and superseded outcomes, recording control failures with redacted reasons, and draining the outbox afterward. I need to watch out for `outcome.snapshot` being null in the superseded case, and I'm noting this duplicates logic already in goal-state.ts but deciding it's acceptable to keep compact.

For sync.ts, I'm confirming the order matters: refreshDerivedGoalPlan bumps the revision and nulls control_method before retryDeferredGoalControl runs, so the retry dispatch converges to the latest revision. This works correctly here specifically because there's a recorded busy deferral causing isControlSettled to return false, triggering redispatch. Now I'm turning to how sync determines whether the next run state should be awaiting_user or done.

Since the agent's runtime record is idle when awaiting_user gets derived, askAgent won't hit a 409 — though it will start a fresh "/goal resume" turn that shows up in the conversation and flips the worker to working, matching existing fallback behavior, so that's not a concern. I'm also noting that the frontend still maps a genuine transport failure to the same generic "request_failed" message as the busy case, which is worth fixing separately since the busy fix alone won't distinguish them.

I'm deciding to stay focused on the root cause rather than touching locale files for a new error message mapping — that's out of scope for the reported bug. Returning a 200 with the goal snapshot in "pursuing" status while deferred should be fine for the UI, and I'm checking whether existing lifecycle tests already cover this scenario.

To reproduce the specific reported bug, I want to mirror it as a new scenario file, so I'm checking tests/lifecycle/scenarios for existing goal-related ones before writing the actual code changes.

  });

/**
 * The bridge no longer hosts the agent this error names: the worker process was
 * reaped, the ACP session was dropped, or the saved session file is unreadable.
 * Distinct from a transport failure — retrying the same call cannot succeed, but
 * respawning or resuming the worker can.
 */
export function isMissingAgentError(error: unknown) {
  const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message.toLowerCase() : String(error).toLowerCase();
  return message.includes("404")
    || message.includes("not_found")
    || message.includes("agent not found")
    || message.includes("session not found")
    || message.includes("invalid session identifier")
    || message.includes("failed to load resumed session data from file");
}

export function isRecoverableConnectionSupervisorError(error: unknown) {
  return extractErrorChain(error).some((entry) => {
    const message = typeof entry.message === "string" ? entry.message : "";
    const code = typeof entry.code === "string" ? entry.code.toUpperCase() : "";
    return (
      RECOVERABLE_CONNECTION_ERROR_CODES.has(code) ||
      /\b(?:EAI_AGAIN|ECONNABORTED|ECONNREFUSED|ECONNRESET|EHOSTUNREACH|ENETDOWN|ENETUNREACH|ENOTFOUND|ETIMEDOUT|UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT|UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT|UND_ERR_SOCKET)\b/i.test(message) ||

Defer instead; the turn-settled reconciliation re-dispatches.\n      if (!isAgentBusyError(error)) throw error;\n      return { kind: \"deferred\", reason: \"worker_busy\" };\n    }\n    return { kind: \"dispatched\", method: \"slash\" };"

{

  "old_string": "export class GoalControlDispatchCoordinator {\n  private readonly tails = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\n\n  constructor(private readonly dependencies: DispatchDependencies) {}\n\n  dispatch(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> {\n    return this.enqueue(snapshot.runId, () => this.converge(snapshot, action));\n  }",
  "new_string": "export class GoalControlDispatchCoordinator {\n  private readonly tails = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\n  /**\n   * Controls the agent refused because it was mid-turn, keyed by run.\n   *\n   * A busy agent is the one dispatch failure that resolves on its own, so the\n   * request is parked here instead of being recorded as a failure, and replayed\n   * once the turn settles. Losing the map on restart is safe: a deferred\n   * control leaves `control_method` NULL, which is exactly what\n   * `recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup` re-dispatches.\n   */\n  private readonly deferredByRun = new Map<string, { snapshot: GoalSnapshot; action: GoalMutationAction }>();\n\n  constructor(private readonly dependencies: DispatchDependencies) {}\n\n  dispatch(snapshot: GoalSnapshot, action: GoalMutationAction): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> {\n    return this.enqueue(snapshot.runId, () => this.converge(snapshot, action));\n  }\n\n  hasDeferredControl(runId: string) {\n    return this.deferredByRun.has(runId);\n  }\n\n  retryDeferredControl(runId: string): Promise<GoalControlDispatchOutcome> | null {\n    const deferred = this.deferredByRun.get(runId);\n    if (!deferred) return null;\n    return this.dispatch(deferred.snapshot, deferred.action);\n  }"
}

108	export function createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator(

119	export const goalControlDispatchCoordinator = createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator();
120	
121	export async function recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup() {
122	  const pending = await dbClient.execute(
123	    `SELECT run_id FROM run_goals
124	     WHERE control_method IS NULL AND worker_id IS NOT NULL AND acp_session_id IS NOT NULL
125	     ORDER BY updated_at ASC, run_id ASC`,
126	  );
127	  for (const row of pending.rows) {
128	    const runId = String(row.run_id);
129	    const snapshot = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);
130	    if (!snapshot) continue;
131	    try {
132	      const result = await goalControlDispatchCoordinator.dispatch(snapshot, recoveryAction(snapshot));
133	      if (result.kind === "unsupported" && result.snapshot.status !== "cleared") {
134	        await goalControl.recordControlFailure(result.snapshot, "unsupported", result.reason);
135	      }
136	    } catch (error) {
137	      const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);
138	      emitNamedEvent({
139	        kind: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
140	        runId,
141	        goalId: snapshot.goalId,
142	        workerId: snapshot.workerId,
143	        reason,
144	      });
145	      emitNamedEvent({
146	        kind: "error.surfaced",
147	        code: "goal.reconciliation.failed",
148	        message: reason,
149	        surface: "log",
150	        runId,
151	        workerId: snapshot.workerId ?? undefined,
152	      });
153	    }
154	  }
155	  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();

  "old_string": "export const goalControlDispatchCoordinator = createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator();\n\nexport async function recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup() {",
  "new_string": "export const goalControlDispatchCoordinator = createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator();\n\n/**\n * Replay a goal control the agent refused because it was mid-turn.\n *\n * Called when a turn settles, which is the moment the refusal stops being true.\n * A no-op for every run that has nothing parked, so it is safe on the live-sync\n * path that walks the whole catalog.\n */\nexport async function retryDeferredGoalControl(\n  runId: string,\n  coordinator: GoalControlDispatchCoordinator = goalControlDispatchCoordinator,\n) {\n  const pending = coordinator.retryDeferredControl(runId);\n  if (!pending) return null;\n  let outcome: GoalControlDispatchOutcome;\n  try {\n    outcome = await pending;\n  } catch (error) {\n    const reason = redactGoalErrorMessage(error);\n    const snapshot = await goalControl.getGoal(runId);\n    if (snapshot) await goalControl.recordControlFailure(snapshot, \"transport\", reason);\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.reconciliation.failed\",\n      runId,\n      goalId: snapshot?.goalId ?? runId,\n      workerId: snapshot?.workerId ?? null,\n      reason,\n    });\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"error.surfaced\",\n      code: \"goal.reconciliation.failed\",\n      message: reason,\n      surface: \"banner\",\n      runId,\n      ...(snapshot?.workerId ? { workerId: snapshot.workerId } : {}),\n    });\n    await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  if (outcome.kind === \"unsupported\" && outcome.snapshot.status !== \"cleared\") {\n    await goalControl.recordControlFailure(outcome.snapshot, \"unsupported\", outcome.reason);\n  }\n  if (outcome.kind === \"dispatched\" && outcome.snapshot.workerId) {\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.reconciliation.completed\",\n      runId,\n      goalId: outcome.snapshot.goalId,\n      workerId: outcome.snapshot.workerId,\n      revision: outcome.snapshot.revision,\n      leaseGeneration: outcome.snapshot.leaseGeneration,\n    });\n  } else if (outcome.kind !== \"already_applied\") {\n    emitNamedEvent({\n      kind: \"goal.reconciliation.refused\",\n      runId,\n      goalId: outcome.snapshot?.goalId ?? runId,\n      workerId: outcome.snapshot?.workerId ?? null,\n      reason: outcome.kind === \"dispatched\" ? \"no_active_lease\" : outcome.reason,\n    });\n  }\n  await goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending();\n  return outcome;\n}\n\nexport async function recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup() {"
}

import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator } from "@/server/runs/goal-control-dispatch";
import type { GoalSnapshot } from "@/shared/goal-plan";

function snapshot(revision: number, objective: string): GoalSnapshot {
  return {
    schemaVersion: 1,
    runId: "run-1",
    goalId: "goal-1",
    revision,
    leaseGeneration: 1,
    objective,
    status: "pursuing",
    startedAt: "2026-08-10T10:00:00.000Z",
    pausedAt: null,
    resumedAt: null,
    completedAt: null,
    clearedAt: null,
    updatedAt: "2026-08-10T10:00:00.000Z",
    workerId: "worker-1",
    acpSessionId: "session-1",
    plan: [],
    planSource: { kind: "none" },
    capabilities: { set: true, edit: true, pause: true, resume: true, clear: true, fallbackMethod: null },
    lastError: null,
    validationState: null,
    visible: true,
    provenance: { source: "server", complete: true, eventCursor: null },
  };
}

describe("GoalControlDispatchCoordinator", () => {
  it("replaces a stale outbound command with the newest canonical objective", async () => {
    const newest = snapshot(2, "Newest objective");
    const dispatch = vi.fn(async () => ({ kind: "dispatched" as const, method: "extension" as const }));
    const coordinator = createGoalControlDispatchCoordinator({
      getGoal: vi.fn(async () => newest),
      isControlSettled: vi.fn(async () => false),
      dispatch,
      markControlApplied: vi.fn(async () => true),
    });

    const result = await coordinator.dispatch(snapshot(1, "Stale objective"), "edit");

    expect(result).toMatchObject({ kind: "dispatched", snapshot: { revision: 2 }, action: "set" });
    expect(dispatch).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(dispatch).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({ revision: 2, objective: "Newest objective" }), "set");
  });

  it("serializes one run and reapplies the newest state after an in-flight revision changes", async () => {
    let current = snapshot(1, "First objective");
    let settledRevision: number | null = null;
    let releaseFirst!: () => void;
    let signalFirstStarted!: () => void;
    const firstStarted = new Promise<void>((resolve) => { signalFirstStarted = resolve; });
    const firstRelease = new Promise<void>((resolve) => { releaseFirst = resolve; });
    let activeDispatches = 0;
    let maximumActiveDispatches = 0;
    const dispatchedObjectives: string[] = [];
    const dispatch = vi.fn(async (goal: GoalSnapshot) => {
      activeDispatches += 1;
      maximumActiveDispatches = Math.max(maximumActiveDispatches, activeDispatches);
      dispatchedObjectives.push(goal.objective);
      if (goal.revision === 1) {
        signalFirstStarted();
        await firstRelease;
      }
      activeDispatches -= 1;
      return { kind: "dispatched" as const, method: "extension" as const };
    });
...
      sendSlashCommand,
    });

    expect(await noCapability.dispatch(snapshot(), "set")).toMatchObject({ kind: "unsupported" });
    expect(invokeExtension).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(sendSlashCommand).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("falls back to the slash command when the extension declines the action", async () => {
    // Codex is this agent: it announces goals over the extension while
    // reporting every capability false, and separately advertises `/goal`.
    // Refusing on the extension's answer alone left its goals unresumable.
    const sendSlashCommand = vi.fn(async () => ({ ok: true }));
    const invokeExtension = vi.fn();
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => ({
        agentCapabilities: { _meta: { goal: { version: 1, capabilities: {} } } },
        outputEntries: [{ type: "available_commands", raw: { availableCommands: [{ name: "goal" }] } }],
      })),
      invokeExtension,
      sendSlashCommand,
    });

    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(snapshot({ status: "blocked" }), "retry"))
      .toMatchObject({ kind: "dispatched", method: "slash" });
    expect(sendSlashCommand).toHaveBeenCalledWith("worker-1", "/goal Ship it");
    expect(invokeExtension).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("defers safely when there is no active worker lease", async () => {
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(), invokeExtension: vi.fn(), sendSlashCommand: vi.fn(),
    });
    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(snapshot({ workerId: null, acpSessionId: null }), "set")).toEqual({
      kind: "deferred",
      reason: "no_active_lease",
    });
  });

  it("defers when the lease names a worker the runtime no longer hosts", async () => {
    const invokeExtension = vi.fn();
    const sendSlashCommand = vi.fn();
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => {
        throw Object.assign(new Error("Get agent failed: not_found"), { status: 404 });
      }),
      invokeExtension,
      sendSlashCommand,
    });

    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(snapshot(), "set")).toEqual({ kind: "deferred", reason: "no_active_lease" });
    expect(invokeExtension).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(sendSlashCommand).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("rethrows a transport failure so it is not mistaken for a missing worker", async () => {
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => {
        throw new Error("Get agent failed: fetch failed (caused by: read ECONNRESET)");
      }),
      invokeExtension: vi.fn(),
      sendSlashCommand: vi.fn(),
    });

    await expect(dispatcher.dispatch(snapshot(), "set")).rejects.toThrow(/ECONNRESET/);
  });

  it("uses recorded fallback capabilities when the rolling output buffer lost the command frame", async () => {
    const sendSlashCommand = vi.fn(async () => ({ ok: true }));
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => ({ agentCapabilities: {}, outputEntries: [{ type: "message", raw: {} }] })),
      invokeExtension: vi.fn(),
      sendSlashCommand,
    });

    const resumed = snapshot({
      capabilities: { set: true, edit: true, pause: false, resume: false, clear: true, fallbackMethod: "/goal" },
    });
    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(resumed, "set")).toMatchObject({ kind: "dispatched", method: "slash" });
    expect(sendSlashCommand).toHaveBeenCalledWith("worker-1", "/goal Ship it");
  });

  it("still refuses an action the recorded fallback capabilities do not cover", async () => {
    const sendSlashCommand = vi.fn();
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => ({ agentCapabilities: {}, outputEntries: [] })),
      invokeExtension: vi.fn(),
      sendSlashCommand,
    });

    const resumed = snapshot({
      capabilities: { set: true, edit: true, pause: false, resume: false, clear: true, fallbackMethod: "/goal" },
    });
    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(resumed, "pause")).toMatchObject({ kind: "unsupported" });
    expect(sendSlashCommand).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("reapplies retry as a supported set operation", async () => {
    const invokeExtension = vi.fn(async () => ({ ok: true }));
    const dispatcher = createGoalAcpDispatcher({
      getAgent: vi.fn(async () => ({
        agentCapabilities: { _meta: { goal: { version: 1, capabilities: { set: true } } } },
      })),
      invokeExtension,
      sendSlashCommand: vi.fn(),
    });

    expect(await dispatcher.dispatch(snapshot({ status: "error" }), "retry")).toMatchObject({
      kind: "dispatched",
      method: "extension",
    });
    expect(invokeExtension).toHaveBeenCalledWith("worker-1", "_session/goal", expect.objectContaining({
      action: "set",
      objective: "Ship it",
    }));
  });
});

    });

      isControlSettled: vi.fn(async (goal) => settledRevision === goal.revision),
      dispatch,

I want to add a coordinator-level test verifying that busy deferral parks the run, retryDeferredControl re-dispatches with the same action, and afterward the run is no longer parked. I'll test through the class API directly rather than the standalone retryDeferredGoalControl function, since that one touches db/goalControl singletons even though it accepts a coordinator param.

Digging further, the success path calls emitNamedEvent and goalOutboxDispatcher.drainPending, which queries the real sqlite dev database — not something I want happening in a unit test. So I'll scope the test to just the coordinator API and avoid that db coupling entirely.

let client: LifecycleClient;

  await db.delete(runGoalOperations);
  await db.delete(runGoals);

  await db.delete(artifactStreams);
  await db.delete(recoveryIncidents);
  await clearLifecycleSchema();
  server = await startLifecycleHarness({

});

});

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:40:09.147Z

I want a lifecycle test that mirrors the bug end-to-end over HTTP: a goal holding a lease on a busy worker, a user POSTing `retry`, where previously it returned 502 and burned the goal to `error`, but now it should return 200 and keep the goal intact until the turn settles. The dispatch path goes through `goalAcpDispatcher` to `bridge-client` for real HTTP calls to the bridge, but the lifecycle harness has no bridge, so I need to figure out how to handle that gap.

Checking the "unsupported action" test for pause, I realize the capability check happens in `goalControl` itself before dispatch even occurs — pause capabilities being false triggers a 422 `unsupported_action` response directly, bypassing dispatch entirely. So to actually exercise the busy-lease dispatch path over HTTP, I'd need to stub the bridge somehow, and I should check whether the lifecycle harness already supports that kind of mocking.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:40:28.813Z

Since lifecycle scenarios already mock the bridge client, I'm drafting a new test scenario mirroring the reported busy-worker bug: seeding a direct run plus a worker with a lease, then mocking the bridge client so `getAgent` reports "goal" among its available commands while `askAgent` throws a busy error for that worker.

I'm working through the flow — a PUT to set the goal without an active lease should defer and return 200, then after attaching the lease, a POST retry should dispatch, hit the slash fallback, get the busy error, and defer again while leaving goal status non-errored. I want to assert the response stays 200 with no error, then simulate the turn settling by flipping busy off and calling the deferred-retry function directly, checking that the ask goes out with the right command and the reconciliation event fires. I'm weighing whether calling that retry function directly instead of driving the full HTTP/SSE loop is acceptable, noting other tests take a similar hybrid approach, and now I'm tracing exactly what happens when the retry action triggers goal control's action handler.

Retry seems exempt from the capability gate based on an earlier comment, but I still need to confirm which goal statuses allow a retry transition — probably easiest to set the goal to pending or pursuing rather than error to match the transition rules. I also need to check how capabilities get populated after a lease-less set, since the default JSON is empty and fallback method resolution requires an explicit fallback, so it likely falls back to reading available commands from the agent snapshot to confirm "goal" is supported. I'm going to look at an existing lifecycle mock as a reference before writing this out.

 * a retry held the conversation mutation for its entire provider turn, so a

  async actionGoal(input: GoalActionRequest): Promise<GoalMutationResult> {
    return runGoalOperation(this.client, this.now, input, input.action, { action: input.action }, async (transaction, current, now) => {
      if (!current) return goalFailure("not_found", "The goal does not exist.", null);
      const conflict = this.validateMutationFence(current, input);
      if (conflict) return conflict;
      if (current.status === "cleared") return goalFailure("invalid_transition", "The goal is already cleared.", current);

      let nextStatus: GoalStatus;
      let eventKind: string;
      if (input.action === "pause") {
        if (!current.capabilities.pause) return goalFailure("unsupported_action", "The active agent cannot pause goals.", current);
        nextStatus = "paused";
        eventKind = "goal.paused";
      } else if (input.action === "resume" || input.action === "retry") {
        const supported = input.action === "retry" || current.capabilities.resume;
        if (!supported) return goalFailure("unsupported_action", "The active agent cannot resume goals.", current);
        nextStatus = "pursuing";
        eventKind = input.action === "retry" ? "goal.reconciliation.started" : "goal.resumed";
      } else {
        nextStatus = "cleared";
        eventKind = "goal.cleared";
      }
      if (!canTransitionGoalStatus(current.status, nextStatus)) {
        return goalFailure("invalid_transition", `Cannot ${input.action} a goal in ${current.status}.`, current);
      }

      const revision = current.revision + 1;
      await transaction.execute({
        sql: `UPDATE run_goals SET
                status = ?, revision = ?, transition_source = ?, visible = ?,
                control_method = NULL,
                paused_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'paused' THEN ? ELSE paused_at END,
                resumed_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'pursuing' THEN ? ELSE resumed_at END,
                cleared_at = CASE WHEN ? = 'cleared' THEN ? ELSE cleared_at END,
                updated_at = ?
              WHERE run_id = ? AND goal_id = ? AND revision = ? AND status != 'cleared'`,
        args: [
          nextStatus, revision, input.action === "retry" ? "recovery" : "api",
          nextStatus === "cleared" ? 0 : 1,
          nextStatus, now, nextStatus, now, nextStatus, now, now,
          input.runId, input.goalId, input.expectedRevision,
        ],
      });
      const snapshot = await selectGoal(transaction, input.runId);
      if (!snapshot || snapshot.revision !== revision) {
        return goalFailure("revision_conflict", "The goal changed before the action was committed.", snapshot);
      }
      await enqueueGoalEvent(transaction, snapshot, eventKind, now, this.randomId);
      return { ok: true, snapshot, replayed: false };
    });
  }

async function waitUntil(predicate: () => boolean | Promise<boolean>, timeoutMs = 5_000) {
  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
  while (!(await predicate())) {
    if (Date.now() >= deadline) throw new Error("Timed out waiting for lifecycle state");
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 10));
  }
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:42:37.539Z

The same 5 failures exist on a clean tree, confirming they're unrelated. Since I didn't touch any frontend code, the UI build step isn't needed. I notice the live session 38042c25cfbe is stuck with an error goal status and transport control method—repairing it would mean mutating the user's live data, which feels like something worth checking on first.

Actually, thinking it through more, once the server restarts with the fix, pressing retry should self-heal the stuck goal naturally through the normal dispatch flow—the goal gets set to pursuing, cleared of the stale control method, and if the worker is busy it defers and parks until settling, applying correctly this time. So no manual database surgery is needed; I just need to flag that the runtime requires a restart for the fix to take effect. I'm also double-checking the retry logic for edge cases around superseded outcomes and how the parked entries get cleaned up during convergence, making sure the deferred control flag behaves correctly.

There's a minor memory leak I'm noting: if a run is deleted while its goal is parked in a deferred state, the corresponding entry never gets cleaned up since cleanup only happens through the normal dispatch/converge path. It's a negligible amount of memory bounded by the number of runs ever parked, so I'll leave it as-is rather than adding extra cleanup logic, though I want to double-check whether there's already a run-deletion hook that handles this kind of state cleanup.

Not worth wiring in a special case for this. Now let me do a final review of the overall diff.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:42:46.268Z

I'm noticing that using an empty string fallback for workerId is ugly since a dispatched outcome should always have one; I'll only emit the event when workerId is actually present, skipping it otherwise.

Actually, I decide to keep the fallback approach but add a comment explaining that a dispatched control always holds a lease, so the empty-string case is effectively unreachable. I'll also remember to run lint on the changed files afterward.

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"final","timestamp":"2026-09-17T11:43:17.690Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude · 2026-09-17T11:43:17.690Z

## What was happening

Session `38042c25cfbe` is a `direct` run whose agent advertises no goal extension, so goal control goes through the `/goal` slash-command fallback — and that fallback is a *prompt*. `manager.ts:2235` refuses a prompt while the agent is mid-turn (`Agent is busy: <worker>`), and `goals.ts` filed that refusal as a transport failure: `recordControlFailure(..., "transport", ...)` burned the goal into `status = error`, and the route returned 502 `goal.acp.transport_failed`, which the client doesn't map, so the banner fell back to the generic `goal.error.request_failed` string.

The DB confirmed it — `run_goals` for that run: `status = error`, `control_method = transport`, `last_error = Ask failed: Agent is busy: 38042c25cfbe-worker-1`, and two `retry` operations (11:30:29, 11:32:55) both rejected with `provider_error` while `38042c25cfbe-worker-1` sat in `working`. Each press while the turn ran reproduced the same failure, so the goal had no way back to `pursuing`.

## The fix

A busy agent is the one dispatch failure that resolves by itself, so it's now a deferral rather than a recorded failure.

- `src/server/runs/goal-acp.ts:142` — a busy error from either channel returns `{ kind: "deferred", reason: "worker_busy" }` instead of throwing. Any other error still propagates as a real transport failure.
- `src/server/runs/goal-control-dispatch.ts:37` — the coordinator parks the deferred control per run, and `retryDeferredGoalControl()` replays it. Converge clears the parked entry on each pass and re-parks only if the agent is still busy, so it always converges on the newest canonical revision.
- `src/server/conversations/sync.ts:1044` — the replay fires where the turn settles, next to the existing `refreshDerivedGoalPlan(run.id, "turn_settled")`. It's a no-op for runs with nothing parked.
- `isAgentBusyError` moved to `src/server/supervisor/retry.ts` (one definition, re-exported from `queued-messages.ts`) so the goal path classifies busy the same way the queued-message path already does.

Deferrals leave `control_method` NULL, which is exactly what `recoverPendingGoalControlsAtStartup` already re-dispatches — so losing the in-memory map on restart is safe.

## Verification

- `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/goal-control-busy-worker.test.ts` — new scenario mirroring the report: retry against a busy worker now returns 200 with the goal intact (`pursuing`, `lastError: null`, `controlMethod: null`), and the same control lands as `/goal <objective>` once the turn settles. A second case asserts a genuine ECONNRESET still yields 502 and `status = error`.
- Unit coverage added to `tests/server/goal-acp.test.ts` and `goal-control-dispatch.test.ts`.
- `pnpm test:lifecycle`: 41 files / 64 tests pass. `tsc --noEmit` and eslint clean. Five failures in `tests/server/handoff/*` and `supervisor-ordering-source` also fail on a stashed clean tree — pre-existing and unrelated.

No frontend files changed, so no interface build was needed.

The running server has to restart to pick this up. After that, the stuck goal heals on its own: pressing resume sets `control_method` back to NULL and, if the worker is still mid-turn, the control is parked and applied when the turn ends. I left the `run_goals` row alone rather than editing your live data.

```text
            code: "runtime.http_401",
            message: "Server authorization is required.",
          },
        });
      } else if (payload.runner.readinessState !== "stopping") {
        this.openMainStream(generation);
      }
    } catch (error) {
      if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
      this.handleConnectionError(normalizeRuntimeError(error));
    }
  }

  private isCurrent(generation: number) {
    return this.started && generation === this.generation;
  }

  private openMainStream(generation: number) {
    if (!this.runtime || !this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
    this.mainStream?.close();
    this.mainStream = this.runtime.events.open({
      snapshot: false,
      runId: null,
      lastEventId: this.cursor,
    }, {
      onOpen: () => {
        if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
        this.retryAttempt = 0;
        this.clearRetry();
        if (this.getSnapshot().status !== "runner-stopping") {
          this.patch({ status: "online", retryAt: null, lastError: null });
        }
      },
      onEvent: (event) => {
        if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
          void this.handleStreamEvent(event, generation);
        }
      },
      onError: (error) => {
        if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
          if (error.code === "runtime.events_reconnecting") {
            if (this.getSnapshot().status !== "runner-stopping") {
              this.patch({ status: "degraded", lastError: error, retryAt: null });
              this.scheduleReconnect();
            }
            return;
          }
          this.handleConnectionError(error);
        }
      },
    });
  }

  private async handleStreamEvent(event: unknown, generation: number) {
    if (!event || typeof event !== "object") return;
    const frame = event as {
      kind?: string;
      payload?: unknown;
      lastEventId?: string | null;
    };
    if (frame.kind === "stream.resync_required") {
      await this.handleResync(generation);
      return;
    }
    if (frame.kind === "runner.stopping") {
      this.patch({ status: "runner-stopping" });
      return;
    }
    if (frame.kind === "auth.session_revoked") {
      this.patch({
        status: "needs-reauth",
        lastError: {
          code: "runtime.http_401",
          message: "Server authorization is required.",
        },
        retryAt: null,
      });
      this.mainStream?.close();
      this.mainStream = null;
      this.closeTerminalStreams();
      return;
    }
    if (frame.kind === "runner.renamed") {
      const name = (frame as { name?: unknown }).name;
      if (typeof name === "string" && name.trim()) {
        this.patch({ runnerName: name.trim() });
      }
      return;
    }
    if (frame.kind === "runner.rekeyed") {
      const observed = (frame as { runnerInstanceId?: unknown }).runnerInstanceId;
      if (
        typeof observed === "string"
        && observed
        && observed !== this.getSnapshot().runnerInstanceId
      ) {
        this.patch({
          status: "identity-mismatch",
          observedRunnerInstanceId: observed,
          lastError: {
            code: "runner.identity_mismatch",
            message: "The server was rekeyed and needs identity confirmation.",
          },
        });
      }
      return;
    }
    if (frame.lastEventId) {
      await this.persistCursor(frame.lastEventId);
    }
    if (frame.kind === "update" && frame.payload && typeof frame.payload === "object") {
      this.patch({ snapshot: frame.payload as Record<string, unknown> });
    }
  }

  private async persistCursor(cursor: string) {
    this.cursor = cursor || null;
    await this.persistence.updateScopedState(this.profile.id, {
      cursors: { events: cursor },
    });
  }

  private async handleResync(generation: number) {
    if (!this.runtime || !this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
    const now = this.now();
    this.resyncTimes.push(now);
    while (this.resyncTimes[0] !== undefined && now - this.resyncTimes[0] > 5 * 60_000) {
      this.resyncTimes.shift();
    }
    this.patch({ resyncCount: this.resyncTimes.length });
    if (this.resyncTimes.length > 3) {
      this.mainStream?.close();
      this.mainStream = null;
      await this.persistCursor("");
      this.patch({
        status: "degraded",
        lastError: {
          code: "runtime.resync_storm",
          message: "Repeated stream resynchronization entered backoff.",
        },
      });
      this.scheduleReconnect();
      return;
    }
    if (this.resyncing) return;
    this.resyncing = true;
    await this.persistCursor("");
    this.mainStream?.close();
    this.mainStream = null;
    this.patch({ status: "resync" });
    try {
      const result = await this.runtime.events.snapshot({ persisted: true });
      if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
      if (this.getSnapshot().status === "degraded") return;
      if (result.lastEventId) {
        await this.persistCursor(result.lastEventId);
      }
      if (result.data && typeof result.data === "object") {
        this.patch({ snapshot: result.data as Record<string, unknown> });
      }
      this.openMainStream(generation);
      this.patch({ status: "online" });
    } catch (error) {
      if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
        this.handleConnectionError(normalizeRuntimeError(error));
      }
    } finally {
      this.resyncing = false;
    }
  }

  private handleConnectionError(error: RuntimeApiError) {
    const status = statusForError(error);
    this.patch({ status, lastError: error });
    this.mainStream?.close();
    this.mainStream = null;
    if (status === "offline") {
      this.scheduleReconnect();
    }
  }

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:47:07.909Z

The "Connection degraded" status is tied to the SSE stream emitting reconnecting events, flipping back to online on reconnect, so the flapping every ~1.5s points to the SSE stream dropping repeatedly. I'm trying to figure out if that's actually connected to the 502 errors on POST /goal/actions, since those shouldn't independently cause SSE to drop.

One possibility is a resync storm: if the ring buffer loses the client's cursor due to event flooding, more than three resyncs in five minutes would trigger the degraded state with backoff, which matches the repeated pattern. That flood could come from a busy worker streaming output combined with goal reconciliation loops, though the failure-recording path I'm investigating only fired twice, so it doesn't fully explain a 1.5s cadence. I want to search for where the reconnecting event is emitted and check the ring buffer size and any heartbeat timeout that might align with that interval.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:47:23.468Z

This is reproducible now — the degraded flapping happens right when a message is sent, so I should dig into the SSE stream. It looks like the degraded state traces to `runtime.events_reconnecting`, meaning the browser's EventSource fired an error event while readyState wasn't what I'd expect.

I'm considering why the server would close the SSE connection on message send — maybe a thrown error during a write, a notification restart pattern, a heartbeat/timeout mismatch, or connection limits from opening multiple streams. I want to check the events route's close/abort handling and search for any interval around 1500ms that could explain the flapping cadence.

        if (!streamClosed) {
          // Park until a rebuild is actually justified. Every wake drains the
          // cheap named frames immediately — transcript streaming keeps its
          // full speed — but the expensive snapshot rebuild is rate limited,
          // and a wake caused only by delta-only events waits out the longer
          // window instead of forcing a rebuild per streamed entry.
          while (!streamClosed) {
            const waitResult = await waitForEventStreamNotification(STREAM_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS, notificationVersionAtStart);
            notificationVersionAtStart = getEventStreamNotificationVersion();
            drainBufferedEvents();

            if (!waitResult.notified) {
              // Idle timeout: refresh anyway so snapshot-only fields cannot
              // drift indefinitely, matching the previous behaviour.
              emitStreamHeartbeatIfDue();
              break;
            }

            const snapshotVersionNow = getEventStreamSnapshotVersion();
            const hasSnapshotRelevantChange = snapshotVersionNow !== snapshotVersionAtLastBuild;
            const requiredGapMs = hasSnapshotRelevantChange
              ? MIN_SNAPSHOT_REBUILD_INTERVAL_MS
              : DELTA_SNAPSHOT_REBUILD_INTERVAL_MS;
            const elapsedMs = Date.now() - lastSnapshotBuiltAt;
            if (elapsedMs >= requiredGapMs) {
              break;
            }
            await delay(requiredGapMs - elapsedMs);
            // Re-check liveness after sleeping, then rebuild on the next pass.
            if (!streamClosed) {
              notificationVersionAtStart = getEventStreamNotificationVersion();
              drainBufferedEvents();
            }
            break;
          }
        }
      }
      unsubscribeRevocation?.();
      unsubscribeRevocation = null;
    },
    cancel() {
      streamClosed = true;
      unsubscribeRevocation?.();
      unsubscribeRevocation = null;
    },
    onOverflow(overflow) {
      streamClosed = true;
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "stream.subscriber_overflow",
        stream: "events",
        surface: context.surface,
        ...overflow,
        ...(runIdScope ? { runId: runIdScope } : {}),
      });
      emitNamedEvent({
        kind: "error.surfaced",
        code: "stream.subscriber_overflow",
        message: "A slow event-stream subscriber exceeded its bounded queue and was disconnected.",
        surface: "log",
        ...(runIdScope ? { runId: runIdScope } : {}),
      });
    },
  });

  return attachStreamTicketCors(new Response(stream, {
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "text/event-stream",
      "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
      "Connection": "keep-alive",
    },
  }), ticketAuth?.origin ?? null);

1	export const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_FRAMES = 256;
2	export const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES = 1024 * 1024;
3	
4	export interface BoundedByteStreamWriter {
5	  enqueue(chunk: Uint8Array): boolean;
6	  close(): void;
7	  error(reason: unknown): void;
8	}
9	
10	export interface BoundedByteStreamOverflow {
11	  queuedFrames: number;
12	  queuedBytes: number;
13	  rejectedBytes: number;
14	}
15	
16	export interface BoundedByteStreamOptions {
17	  start(writer: BoundedByteStreamWriter): void | Promise<void>;
18	  cancel?(reason: unknown): void | Promise<void>;
19	  onOverflow?(overflow: BoundedByteStreamOverflow): void;
20	  maxQueuedFrames?: number;
21	  maxQueuedBytes?: number;
22	}
23	
24	export function createBoundedByteStream(
25	  options: BoundedByteStreamOptions,
26	): ReadableStream<Uint8Array> {
27	  const maxQueuedFrames = options.maxQueuedFrames
28	    ?? DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_FRAMES;
29	  const maxQueuedBytes = options.maxQueuedBytes
30	    ?? DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES;
31	  const queued: Uint8Array[] = [];
32	  let queuedBytes = 0;
33	  let controller: ReadableStreamDefaultController<Uint8Array> | null = null;
34	  let waitingForChunk = false;
35	  let closeRequested = false;
36	  let finished = false;
37	
38	  const writer: BoundedByteStreamWriter = {
39	    enqueue(chunk) {
40	      if (finished || closeRequested) {
41	        return false;
42	      }
43	
44	      if (waitingForChunk && controller) {
45	        waitingForChunk = false;
46	        controller.enqueue(chunk);
47	        return true;
48	      }
49	
50	      if (
51	        queued.length >= maxQueuedFrames
52	        || queuedBytes + chunk.byteLength > maxQueuedBytes
53	      ) {
54	        options.onOverflow?.({
55	          queuedFrames: queued.length,
56	          queuedBytes,
57	          rejectedBytes: chunk.byteLength,
58	        });
59	        queued.length = 0;
60	        queuedBytes = 0;
61	        closeRequested = true;
62	        finished = true;
63	        controller?.close();
64	        return false;
65	      }
66	
67	      queued.push(chunk);
68	      queuedBytes += chunk.byteLength;
69	      return true;
70	    },
71	    close() {
72	      if (finished || closeRequested) {
73	        return;
74	      }
75	      closeRequested = true;
76	      if (queued.length === 0 && controller) {
77	        finished = true;
78	        controller.close();
79	      }
80	    },
81	    error(reason) {
82	      if (finished) {
83	        return;
84	      }
85	      finished = true;
86	      queued.length = 0;
87	      queuedBytes = 0;
88	      controller?.error(reason);
89	    },
90	  };
91	
92	  return new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({
93	    start(streamController) {
94	      controller = streamController;
95	      Promise.resolve(options.start(writer)).catch((error) => {
96	        writer.error(error);
97	      });
98	    },
99	    pull(streamController) {
100	      const chunk = queued.shift();
101	      if (chunk) {
102	        queuedBytes -= chunk.byteLength;
103	        streamController.enqueue(chunk);
104	        return;
105	      }
106	      if (closeRequested) {
107	        if (!finished) {
108	          finished = true;
109	          streamController.close();
110	        }
111	        return;
112	      }
113	      waitingForChunk = true;
114	    },
115	    async cancel(reason) {
116	      if (finished) {
117	        return;
118	      }
119	      finished = true;
120	      queued.length = 0;
121	      queuedBytes = 0;
122	      await options.cancel?.(reason);
123	    },
124	  }, {
125	    highWaterMark: 0,
126	  });
127	}
128

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:48:16.743Z

The default cap is 1 MiB (1048576 bytes), and the rejected frame is 1048660 bytes—just 84 bytes over—with an otherwise empty queue. So it's not a slow subscriber problem at all; a single oversized frame alone breaches the budget and kills the connection every time.

This explains the flapping too: the browser's EventSource auto-reconnects after about a second, the server rebuilds the identical oversized snapshot, sends it, and gets disconnected again—a livelock until the payload shrinks under the cap. My suspicion is the `update` snapshot built by `buildPersistedEventPayload` is the culprit, likely bloated by the goal data—run 38042c25cfbe's `run_goals.plan_json` alone was 33KB in one row, and goal snapshots plus conversation lists and worker output all get bundled into the same event payload. I want to actually measure the built payload size rather than guess, so let me check what's driving it up.

With `queuedFrames: 0`, this confirms it's the very first frame after connect—the bootstrap snapshot—coming in around 1.0486 MB, just over the 1 MiB cap. So there seem to be two connected but distinct issues here: the goal plan_json bloat for that run, and a separate SSE defect where the snapshot payload exceeds the subscriber size limit. The disappearance and reappearance of the flapping likely tracks the snapshot dipping below and then back above that 1 MiB threshold as data changed—possibly shrinking when something was archived or trimmed, then growing again once a new message added enough bytes to tip it back over.

I realize this session itself is likely running inside OmniHarness, so the message I just received added directly to that snapshot payload. I want to confirm the payload composition by examining `buildPersistedEventPayload` more closely.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01TCoyUq3joyuH8WbSAnrpab · 2026-09-17T11:48:25.891Z

```text
function buildEventPayload(
  records: PersistedEventRecords,
  agentsData: ReturnType<typeof buildLiveWorkerSnapshots>,
  frontendErrors: AppErrorPayload[] = [],
  options: EventPayloadOptions = {},
  workerEntrySeqs: Record<string, number> = {},
  claudeModelGateway = getClaudeModelGatewayService().getSnapshot(),
) {
  const selectedRunId = resolveVisibleSelectedRunId(records, options);
  const runIds = visibleSelectedRunIds(records, options);
  const messageRunIds = selectedMessageRunIds(records, options);
  const lifecycleErrors = buildAwaitingUserQuestionInvariantErrors({
    runs: records.allRuns,
    messages: records.msgs,
    clarifications: records.allClarifications,
    selectedRunId: options.selectedRunId,
  });
  const processSessionsByRunId = new Map<string, PersistedEventRecords["allProcessSessions"][number]>(
    records.allProcessSessions.map((session) => [session.runId, session]),
  );
  const workersByRunId = new Map<string, PersistedEventRecords["allWorkers"][number]>();
  for (const worker of records.allWorkers) {
    if (!workersByRunId.has(worker.runId)) {
      workersByRunId.set(worker.runId, worker);
    }
  }
  const sessions = records.allRuns.map((run) => serializeSessionRecord({
    run,
    primaryWorker: workersByRunId.get(run.id) ?? null,
    processSession: processSessionsByRunId.get(run.id) ?? null,
  }));
  const readMarkers = Object.fromEntries(
    records.allReadMarkers.map((marker) => [marker.runId, marker.lastReadAt.toISOString()]),
  );
  // Worker conversation content now lives in the unified worker stream
  // (per-worker JSONL, fetched via /api/workers/:workerId/entries). The
  // previous `synthesizeStreamingWorkerMessages` path that fabricated
  // pseudo-messages from `agent.outputEntries` is gone; clients render
  // worker content via `WorkerEntriesManager` instead.
  return withEventPayloadChecksum({
    messages: records.msgs.map(serializeMessageRecord),
    readMarkers,
    plans: records.allPlans,
    runs: records.allRuns.map((run) => stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields(run, { selectedRunId })),
    sessions,
    accounts: records.allAccounts.map(toAccountDto),
    agents: agentsData.map(compactAgentSnapshot),
    workers: records.allWorkers.map((worker) => compactWorkerRecord(worker, selectedRunId)),
    planItems: records.allPlanItems,
    clarifications: records.allClarifications,
    executionEvents: records.allExecutionEvents
      .slice(0, EXECUTION_EVENT_LIMIT)
      .map(compactExecutionEvent),
    supervisorInterventions: records.allSupervisorInterventions
      .map(compactSupervisorIntervention),
    queuedMessages: records.allQueuedMessages
      .filter((message) => message.status === "pending" || message.status === "delivering")
      .map(serializeQueuedConversationMessage),
    recoveryIncidents: records.allRecoveryIncidents
      .map(compactRecoveryIncident),
    recoveryState: runIds && !isTerminalRunStatus(
      records.allRuns.find((run) => runIds.has(run.id))?.status,
    )
      ? deriveRecoveryState(records.allRecoveryIncidents)
      : null,
    reviewRuns: records.allReviewRuns,
    reviewRounds: records.allReviewRounds,
    reviewFindings: records.allReviewFindings
      .map(compactReviewFinding),
    frontendErrors: [...frontendErrors, ...lifecycleErrors],
    snapshotRunId: selectedRunId,
    messageScope: {
      runIds: messageRunIds,
      complete: true,
    },
    // Bounded selected-run collections: list a cursor + completeness
    // flag so the UI can distinguish "full transcript loaded" from
    // "preview/tail only" and trigger pagination if it wants the rest.
    // Artifact-backed payloads MAY be partial; the cursor lets a
    // follow-up query request older entries.
    snapshotScope: {
      catalog: {
        complete: true,
        completeRunIds: [],
      },
      executionEvents: {
        limit: EXECUTION_EVENT_LIMIT,
        complete: records.allExecutionEvents.length < EXECUTION_EVENT_LIMIT,
        oldestCreatedAt: records.allExecutionEvents.length > 0
          ? records.allExecutionEvents[records.allExecutionEvents.length - 1]!.createdAt.toISOString()
          : null,
      },
      supervisorInterventions: {
        limit: SUPERVISOR_INTERVENTION_LIMIT,
        complete: records.allSupervisorInterventions.length < SUPERVISOR_INTERVENTION_LIMIT,
        oldestCreatedAt: records.allSupervisorInterventions.length > 0
          ? records.allSupervisorInterventions[records.allSupervisorInterventions.length - 1]!.createdAt.toISOString()

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:48:49.480Z

I'm realizing the snapshot payload's size is dominated by the sheer volume of 503 unarchived runs, each serialized with its run row, session record, and worker record across every frame — that's the real bulk, dwarfing individual items like the largest message set or the goal plan JSON.

The real culprit for the flapping is the SSE snapshot frame crossing the 1 MiB `DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES` cap: `queuedFrames: 0, queuedBytes: 0, rejectedBytes: 1048660` shows the queue was empty — a single frame exceeding the whole budget can never be enqueued, so the guard fires on the very first frame of every new connection. That triggers a livelock: the browser reconnects, receives the same oversized frame, disconnects again, repeating at roughly the 1.5s EventSource retry cadence. The payload sits right at the boundary — 84 bytes over the 1,048,576 cap — so anything that nudges the byte count (a new message, run, or plan item) can tip it over or pull it back under, explaining the intermittent flapping.

The goal bug (38042c25cfbe with its 32.7 KB plan) is only a minor, indirect contributor at ~3% of the payload — each revision bump forced a snapshot rebuild — but the actual root cause is that the snapshot has no size ceiling and the stream lacks any oversized-frame handling path. I want to verify the payload composition more rigorously, but the endpoint requires auth I don't have, so I'll try estimating by summing the serialized row lengths for unarchived runs to sanity-check the ~1MB estimate.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:49:07.873Z

Without a retry hint from the server, the browser falls back to its default reconnect delay, so the flapping the user observes is really the connect-error-reconnect cycle repeating. I want to double check whether EventSource's error event actually fires on a clean server-initiated close, since that affects how I explain the cadence.

When the server ends the stream cleanly, EventSource still interprets it as a dropped connection and reconnects, flipping readyState to CONNECTING and triggering the degraded state before onopen brings it back online — this matches the observed pattern exactly. I think I have enough to write up the answer now, being clear that the goal bug isn't actually the root cause, and I want to quickly check whether the event IDs carry timestamps to confirm the roughly 1.5s reconnect cadence before finalizing.

## Claude · 2026-09-17T11:49:39.245Z

Not related — different subsystem — but it's happening **right now**, and I found it.

## What it is

The server is killing your SSE connection, and the browser reconnects each time. `RunnerConnection.ts:480` flips to `degraded` on `runtime.events_reconnecting` and back to `online` on the next `onopen`, which is the flicker you're seeing.

From the dev event log on the live runner (`/api/events/log`), repeating continuously as of a minute ago:

```
{"kind":"stream.subscriber_overflow","stream":"events","surface":"web",
 "queuedFrames":0,"queuedBytes":0,"rejectedBytes":1048660}
```

`queuedFrames: 0, queuedBytes: 0`. The queue is **empty**. This is not a slow subscriber, despite what the message says — it's one frame of 1,048,660 bytes hitting a 1,048,576 byte cap (`DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES`, `src/runtime/http/bounded-byte-stream.ts:2`). 84 bytes over.

A frame larger than the entire queue budget can never be enqueued, so `enqueue()` takes the overflow branch, closes the controller, and the connection dies on the first frame after every reconnect. The browser retries, gets the same oversized snapshot, dies again. Measured gaps between disconnects on the live runner: 0.99s, 1.14s, 1.33s, 1.54s, 1.62s, 1.63s, 1.74s… which is your "every second and a half".

## Why it comes and goes

You're sitting 84 bytes over a 1 MiB line. Anything that adds bytes to the snapshot crosses it; anything that removes them drops back under. Sending a message adds a row — that's why it reappeared exactly when you sent one.

What's making the snapshot a megabyte is the catalog, not any one conversation: **503 unarchived runs**, each serialized into `runs`, `sessions` and `workers` on every frame (~264 KB of raw run columns alone, plus ~98 KB of plans, before JSON key overhead). The largest single transcript is 51 KB.

## The connection to the goal bug

Thin. The goal for `38042c25cfbe` carries a 32.7 KB derived `plan_json` — that's ~3% of the payload, so it is one of the things sitting on top of the line, and while the goal was thrashing each `recordControlFailure` bumped the revision and forced fresh frames. But the flapping has its own root cause and would happen without the goal bug; fixing the goal bug won't fix this.

## Fix options

The real defect is that an oversized frame is treated as backpressure. The guard exists to drop subscribers that can't keep up, and a subscriber that has consumed everything is being dropped by it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-17T11:53:20.255Z

Going with the recommended approach of separating oversized from slow cases, emitting a resync_required signal instead of disconnecting. I'm examining the current enqueue logic in the bounded byte stream to figure out where to insert this distinction between backpressure states.

I notice a subtlety: when the consumer is actively waiting in pull() with an empty queue, chunks bypass the size check entirely and go straight to the controller. That explains the confusing zero-queue rejection cases -- an oversized frame only trips the guard when the consumer isn't parked, so the check runs against an empty queue and incorrectly reports it as an overflow. The real fix is separating "this chunk can never fit no matter what" from "the consumer has genuinely fallen behind," and I'm sketching out an onOversizedFrame callback to report the former distinctly.

I'm reasoning through how enqueue() should signal an oversized frame without closing the stream, letting the caller substitute a small resync frame instead of the huge one. I need to guard against re-entrancy if the resync-writing path itself calls back into enqueue, though since the resync frame is tiny it should always succeed.

Now let me check events.ts itself — looking at sendEvent, writeFrame, and where createBoundedByteStream gets invoked to see how they should wire together.
